# AUDIO-005 Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** `Render MP4` in the browser and `npm run video` write the plan's mix as H.264 + AAC-LC 48 kHz stereo 128 kb/s whenever the Story has audio, refuse to start with a missing, undecodable or not-yet-ready audio source, and keep the v0.3 video-only output for Stories without audio.

**Architecture:** The CLI half comes first and does not depend on AUDIO-004: `scripts/audioRender.ts` stages the bundled files next to the local ones, measures every staged file, compiles the plan with the very sources Remotion will read and decodes each staged file once with the ffmpeg that ships with Remotion; `renderStory` passes the audio props and the AAC arguments. The browser half adds a pure render readiness (read before the click from the page's inputs and AUDIO-004's bundled-audio store), an audio budget, a per-`storyHasAudio` capability probe, an `AudioDecodeCache` and an audio render preparation (frozen sources, re-check, preflight) inside the existing transaction, audio options in `renderStoryMediaOnWeb`, and a post-render MP4 check; the last tasks switch it on in `App`, remove the AUDIO-001 interim block everywhere and verify real renders under system Chrome.

**Tech Stack:** TypeScript (Node `--experimental-strip-types`), React 19, `@remotion/web-renderer` 4.0.529 and `@remotion/media` 4.0.529, mediabunny 1.56.1 (already installed), the ffmpeg/ffprobe of `@remotion/compositor-*`, `node:test`, Playwright (bundled Chromium and system Chrome). No dependency is added.

**Spec:** `docs/audio/AUDIO-005-rendering.md`, `docs/audio/README.md` (AINV-n, constants, D-n, S-n) and the v0.3 specs `docs/assets/ASSET-004-preview-and-browser-rendering.md` and `ASSET-005-cli-local-assets.md` (executors read the spec and the README first; only the code on `main` plus AUDIO-004 is authoritative where a spec sentence is outdated, see the rulings).

**Prerequisite (merged before this branch starts):** AUDIO-001 to AUDIO-004. From AUDIO-004 this plan uses `src/web/audio/bundledAudio.ts` exactly as `docs/audio/AUDIO-004-editor-ui.md` specifies: `requestBundledAudio(ids: Iterable<BundledAudio>)`, `retryBundledAudio(id: BundledAudio)`, `subscribeBundledAudio(listener): () => void`, `getBundledAudioStates(): ReadonlyMap<BundledAudio, BundledAudioState>` (a new map on every transition), `useBundledAudioStates()`, and the states `loading` / `retrying{attempt}` / `ready{peaks, blob, url}` / `fetch-failed{message}` / `unusable{message}`. After AUDIO-004, `App.tsx` already requests the Story's bundled ids, holds `const bundledStates = useBundledAudioStates();`, builds `audioSources` for the Player and the Player uses `bundledAudioPolicy: "explicit"`. The Retry button of a failed bundled sound has the accessible name `Retry loading <label>`; the editor controls are the comboboxes `Music`, `Sound effect`, `Voice`, the sliders `Music volume`, `Music ducking`, `Sound effect volume`, `Voice volume`, the number inputs `Fade in (seconds)` and `Fade out (seconds)`, the checkbox `Auto (from voice)` and the card button `Play <label>`. Tasks 1 to 12 do not touch `App.tsx` and run without AUDIO-004's code except where a task says so (Tasks 1 to 5 do not need it at all).

## Global Constraints

- TypeScript imports use explicit `.ts`/`.tsx` extensions; Node tests are `tests/*.test.mjs` with `node:test` + `node:assert/strict`, importing `.ts` directly (`node --experimental-strip-types --test <file>`). Node cannot import `.tsx`: `App.tsx` is pinned by source-contract tests. Type-only imports use `import type`. Playwright's loader cannot import a `.ts` file: nothing under `tests/browser/**` or `tests/helpers/*.mjs` that Playwright loads may import `.ts` (this is why `scripts/ffmpegBinary.mjs` is plain JavaScript).
- Write the failing test first. After every task: `npm test`, `npm run lint` (eslint + `tsc`), `npm run web:build`, `npx playwright test --project=chromium` (the Playwright web server serves the BUILT bundle: run `npm run web:build` before browser tests). Tasks that touch `scripts/**`, `stories/**` or `tests/cli-local-assets.e2e.mjs` also run `npm run test:cli-e2e`; Tasks 13 and 14 also run `npx playwright test --config=playwright.browser-render.config.mjs` and `npm run test:harness`. Bundled Chromium has no H.264 or AAC encode/decode.
- AINV-6: a Story without audio behaves byte-identically to `main`: CLI props exactly `{story}` (plus `localAssetSources` for image refs as today), the CLI argument list exactly the v0.3 one, browser `muted: true` and the v0.3 render options, no post-render check. Existing expectations for silent Stories are never edited. The only existing assertions this plan edits are those that name something it deliberately changes (listed in the task that changes them): the two AUDIO-001 interim-block tests, the two `AUDIO-001 browser render uses the exact compiled timeline` tests, the two AUDIO-003 audio-budget tests of `tests/local-asset-state.test.mjs`, the list of `storyAudioDurations(` callers, and the Chromium test of the interim banner.
- Output audio (AINV-9): AAC-LC, 48 kHz, stereo, 128 kb/s, in the browser and the CLI. Constants already on `main` (never redefine): `OUTPUT_AUDIO_SAMPLE_RATE = 48_000`, `OUTPUT_AUDIO_BITRATE = 128_000` (`src/audio/constants.ts`), `MAX_BROWSER_STORY_AUDIO_REFS = 64`, `MAX_BROWSER_STORY_AUDIO_BYTES = 256 * 1024 * 1024`, `AUDIO_TRUNCATION_TOLERANCE_SECONDS = 0.25` (`src/localAssets/limits.ts`), `MAX_AUDIO_STORY_SECONDS = 3_600`, `MAX_BROWSER_TOTAL_FRAMES = 9_000` (`src/web/browserPolicy.ts`). The D-9 truncation rule is `isDecodeComplete` (`src/audio/decodeCheck.ts`) everywhere.
- Messages, verbatim: `Audio is still loading.`; `Audio could not be loaded. Retry it in the editor.`; `Audio file <label> cannot be used for rendering.`; `This Story is longer than 300 s with its voices (N s).`; `This Story uses more than 64 audio files.`; `This Story's audio is larger than 256 MiB.`; `This browser cannot encode audio. Use the local CLI: npm run video -- <story.yaml>`; `Bundled audio <id> is not available in this build.`; `Bundled audio <id> does not match the catalog.`.
- Dependencies: nothing is added. Never run anything against production or `*.netlify.app`; never kill processes by image name; commits end with `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>`. The repository ffmpeg is a minimal build: it has the `wav` and `null` muxers but no raw `f32le`/`s16le` muxer, no `anoisesrc`, no filters beyond `sine`, `pan`, `volume`, `aformat`, `aresample` and the few the fixtures use, and no Vorbis encoder.
- Windows dev machine (PowerShell 5.1 for the user; Git Bash for agents); CI on ubuntu-latest, Node 22, system Chrome for `playwright.browser-render.config.mjs` and `TORA_REMOTION_BROWSER_EXECUTABLE=/usr/bin/google-chrome` for the CLI e2e.
- On the Windows checkout the existing source files have CRLF line endings; edit them with the Edit tool (which keeps them) and do not reformat or convert whole files. New files may use LF. Every `Edit` block below lists, per change, the file's current text (*Old*, unique in the file) and its replacement (*New*). Where a task edits `src/web/App.tsx` and AUDIO-004 changed the neighbouring lines, apply the same intent to the equivalent lines without altering AUDIO-004's code.

## Review Focus

1. **A render that starts with audio nobody checked.** A local audio file whose metadata parses but whose samples do not decode (the truncated MP3 declares 10 s and decodes about 6 s); a bundled sound whose fetch is still pending, failed or returned undecodable bytes; a source deleted between the click and the render. A reasonable person expects the render to refuse with a sentence naming the sound, before OPFS (browser) or Remotion (CLI) is touched, and the Render button to enable by itself when the sound becomes ready. Pinned in Tasks 4, 5 (CLI), 7, 10, 13 and 14.
2. **A sound silently missing from the MP4** (INV-5/AINV-5): a `local:audio:` ref or a bundled id without a resolved source, a ready source whose duration was never measured, a bundled file that is absent from `public/audio/` or has no file (`calm-loop`). Expected: the render refuses and says which sound and role. Pinned in Tasks 4, 5, 10 and 11.
3. **A silent Story changing at all** (AINV-6): extra props, audio arguments, a muted flag flipped, an audio probe paid, a post-render check. Pinned in Tasks 3, 5, 8, 11, 12 and 13.
4. **An MP4 that is not the promised one**: HE-AAC, another sample rate, mono, an audio that outlives the video or carries no data, a wrong frame count. Expected: the file is refused before the download with a sentence naming the difference. Pinned in Tasks 12 and 14.
5. **Leaks and half-cleanups**: a staged audio file left in the temporary directory after a failure or Ctrl+C, an object URL or lease not released after a failed preflight, a decode that keeps running for a cancelled render, a stale decode result cached after a library change. Pinned in Tasks 5 (CLI), 9 and 10.
6. **A Story whose exact length is over the browser cap** while its `auto` lower bound passed acceptance: it must not be rejected into recovery at start-up, and the render must block with the exact-length sentence. Pinned in Tasks 7 and 11.

## Rulings assumed by this plan (the controller's, 2026-10-14, plus the points where this plan corrects the spec; each is repeated where it matters)

- Execution order: AUDIO-004 is implemented and merged first. Tasks 1 to 5 (CLI) come first and need none of it; the browser tasks read AUDIO-004's `bundledAudio.ts` through the structural type `BundledAudioStateLike` / `BundledRenderState`, so their Node tests do not import the store.
- `disallowFallbackToHtml5Audio`: the rule is that a render must not silently switch decode path. It is `true` exactly while Remotion renders (`useRemotionEnvironment().isRendering`, which is true for the CLI render and for the browser renderer), `false` for the Player and Studio. The browser renderer never falls back anyway (it fails with a client-side error); the CLI otherwise would fall back to `<Html5Audio>` and ffmpeg. Measured on this machine: a local M4A voice still renders with Remotion's bundled Chrome with the flag set.
- The CLI decode preflight cannot use the spec's `-f f32le -`: that muxer does not exist in the repository ffmpeg ("Requested output format 'f32le' is not known"). It runs `ffmpeg -v error -nostdin -i file:<path> -map 0:a:0 -ac 1 -ar 48000 -t 301 -c:a pcm_s16le -f wav -` and counts the bytes after the WAV header (`decodedSeconds = bytes / (2 x 48_000)`), same 60 s timeout, same D-9 rule. Hardening (controller review): no `-xerror` (the preflight fails only on a non-zero exit, on a decode that produced nothing, on output past 301 s or on the truncation rule, so it accepts what the browser import accepts), a `file:` prefix so a path can never parse as an option, `-t 301` and a byte cap on stdout, and the ffmpeg child is attached to the command's interrupt guard so Ctrl+C and SIGTERM kill it.
- The compositor-package lookup moves to `scripts/ffmpegBinary.mjs` with a hand-written `ffmpegBinary.d.mts` (not `.ts`, as the spec says): Playwright's loader fails on a `.ts` file imported from `tests/helpers/ffmpeg.mjs`, which the browser specs use. The helper re-exports it.
- There is no in-product post-render check on `main`: the spec's "existing download check" is the golden test's ffprobe assertions. `src/web/renderedMp4Check.ts` is new and runs, before the download, only for Stories with audio (silent Stories are unchanged, AINV-6). Its video checks are H.264, 1080 x 1920, about 30 FPS and a frame count equal to the plan's `T`.
- Readiness precedence (Task 7): a terminal problem (a bundled `unusable`, a local ref that is missing, corrupt or unavailable) first, then `fetch-failed`, then loading, then the exact length. In `App` a local-asset block (including the reworded `describeLocalAssetRenderBlock`) is shown before an audio block. The "N s" of the length sentence is the exact total rounded up to whole seconds.
- The audio budget (Task 6) is evaluated beside the image budget inside `resolveStoryLocalAssets`; the state's `message` joins both sentences when both fail. Two AUDIO-003 tests that encoded "audio is not budgeted yet" (70 audio refs; 300 MiB of audio) are updated to the new limits.
- The preflight names a local file by its library label (read from the store only when a preflight fails) and a bundled sound by its catalog label (`Ding`); the readiness sentence for an `unusable` bundled sound uses the id, as the spec says. The preflight failure is a `failure` outcome (the preparation rejects), a source that changed is `assets-changed` (the preparation returns `{ok: false}`).
- Capability: `canRenderMediaOnWeb` already registers the web-renderer's WASM AAC encoder itself when the browser has no native one and only then decides (pinned by a test on the library source); the plan passes `audioCodec: "aac"` and `audioBitrate: OUTPUT_AUDIO_BITRATE` and maps an error limited to `audio-codec-unsupported` to `{kind: "audio-unsupported"}`. **WASM AAC ignores the requested bitrate (measured 67-89 kb/s); accepted** (controller ruling: D-13 treats the WASM encoder as a valid path, cost if wrong: lower-bitrate audio from browsers without native AAC). The in-product post-render check therefore has no bitrate range (the audio only has to be AAC-LC, 48 kHz, stereo, within the duration tolerance and carry data); the 96 to 160 kb/s range is asserted only by the tests of the native-AAC system-Chrome path and the CLI. Native AAC in system Chrome 155 on Windows: supported, output 128 kb/s.
- Confirmed by the controller: the CLI decode preflight uses WAV on stdout instead of the spec's `f32le`; the lookup is `scripts/ffmpegBinary.mjs` + `ffmpegBinary.d.mts`; `src/web/renderedMp4Check.ts` is new and applies only to Stories with audio. All three are repeated in the spec's implementation notes (Task 14).
- Controller review rulings: readiness takes a local voice's duration from its verified payload (the render's own), so a failed read of the page's duration map cannot leave "Audio is still loading." forever; `AudioDecodeCache` times each decode out after 30 s; the audio budget hides the Player exactly like the image budget does (ASSET-004, noted); the Chrome specs assert the 128 kb/s range only when the page's Chrome encodes AAC natively (the ubuntu Chrome of CI probably does not, and takes the accepted WASM path); `disallowFallbackToHtml5Audio` on Linux is accepted and exercised by CI's `test:cli-e2e`; CI `timeout-minutes` goes from 15 to 25.
- The repository ffprobe prints the AAC profile as `1` (LC), not `LC`: the tests accept both.
- Test placement: the spec lists extensions of existing files; the new unit tests live in new files named after what they cover (the AUDIO-003 precedent). The browser specs of the spec's `browser-rendering.spec.mjs` list run under system Chrome in the new `tests/browser/audio-rendering.spec.mjs` (added to `playwright.browser-render.config.mjs` and ignored by `playwright.config.mjs`).

### Task 1: Measuring the sound of an MP4 (`tests/helpers/audioEnvelope.mjs`)

AUDIO-006 builds its parity and golden measurements on these two functions; this plan uses them in Tasks 5 and 14.

**Files:**
- Create: `tests/helpers/audioEnvelope.mjs`, `tests/audio-envelope.test.mjs`
- Modify: `tests/helpers/ffmpeg.mjs` (one new export, `runFfmpeg`)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `resolveFfmpegBinaries()` (inside `tests/helpers/ffmpeg.mjs`).
- Produces:
  - `tests/helpers/ffmpeg.mjs`: `runFfmpeg(args: string[], options?): Promise<{stdout: string; stderr: string}>` (the repository ffmpeg, rejects on a non-zero exit).
  - `tests/helpers/audioEnvelope.mjs`: `ENVELOPE_SAMPLE_RATE = 48_000`; `decodeMp4AudioMono(path: string): Promise<Float32Array>` (the first audio stream of any media file as mono floats at 48 kHz; rejects with `decodeMp4AudioMono failed on <path>: ...`); `goertzelWindows(samples: Float32Array, hz: number, windowMs: number, sampleRate = 48_000): number[]` (the amplitude of `hz` in consecutive non-overlapping windows; a full-scale sine reads about 1; a trailing partial window is dropped).

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

Create `tests/audio-envelope.test.mjs`:

```js
import assert from "node:assert/strict";
import {mkdtemp, rm} from "node:fs/promises";
import {tmpdir} from "node:os";
import {join} from "node:path";
import {after, before, describe, it} from "node:test";
import {
  decodeMp4AudioMono,
  ENVELOPE_SAMPLE_RATE,
  goertzelWindows,
} from "./helpers/audioEnvelope.mjs";
import {runFfmpeg} from "./helpers/ffmpeg.mjs";

const tone = (hz, seconds, amplitude = 1, startSeconds = 0, totalSeconds = seconds + startSeconds) => {
  const samples = new Float32Array(Math.round(totalSeconds * ENVELOPE_SAMPLE_RATE));

  for (let index = 0; index < samples.length; index += 1) {
    const time = index / ENVELOPE_SAMPLE_RATE;

    if (time >= startSeconds && time < startSeconds + seconds) {
      samples[index] = amplitude * Math.sin(2 * Math.PI * hz * time);
    }
  }

  return samples;
};

const mix = (...signals) => {
  const out = new Float32Array(Math.max(...signals.map((signal) => signal.length)));

  for (const signal of signals) {
    for (let index = 0; index < signal.length; index += 1) {
      out[index] += signal[index];
    }
  }

  return out;
};

const near = (actual, expected, tolerance) =>
  assert.ok(
    Math.abs(actual - expected) <= tolerance,
    `${actual} is not within ${tolerance} of ${expected}`,
  );

describe("goertzelWindows on synthetic tones", () => {
  it("reads the amplitude of a tone per 100 ms window", () => {
    const windows = goertzelWindows(tone(440, 1, 0.5), 440, 100);

    assert.equal(windows.length, 10);

    for (const amplitude of windows) {
      near(amplitude, 0.5, 0.01);
    }
  });

  it("reads ~0 for other frequencies and for silence", () => {
    const samples = tone(440, 1, 1);

    for (const hz of [220, 880, 1000]) {
      for (const amplitude of goertzelWindows(samples, hz, 100)) {
        assert.ok(amplitude < 0.02, `${hz} Hz read ${amplitude}`);
      }
    }

    for (const amplitude of goertzelWindows(new Float32Array(48_000), 440, 100)) {
      assert.equal(amplitude, 0);
    }
  });

  it("follows a tone that starts and stops, window by window", () => {
    // 0.5 s of silence, 0.5 s of 440 Hz, 0.5 s of silence.
    const windows = goertzelWindows(tone(440, 0.5, 0.8, 0.5, 1.5), 440, 100);

    assert.equal(windows.length, 15);
    windows.slice(0, 5).forEach((amplitude) => assert.ok(amplitude < 0.01));
    windows.slice(5, 10).forEach((amplitude) => near(amplitude, 0.8, 0.01));
    windows.slice(10).forEach((amplitude) => assert.ok(amplitude < 0.01));
  });

  it("tells simultaneous tones apart (music 220 Hz under a voice 440 Hz)", () => {
    const samples = mix(tone(220, 1, 0.4), tone(440, 1, 0.2));

    near(goertzelWindows(samples, 220, 100)[5], 0.4, 0.01);
    near(goertzelWindows(samples, 440, 100)[5], 0.2, 0.01);
    assert.ok(goertzelWindows(samples, 880, 100)[5] < 0.02);
  });

  it("drops a trailing partial window and rejects unusable arguments", () => {
    assert.equal(goertzelWindows(tone(440, 0.25), 440, 100).length, 2);
    assert.throws(() => goertzelWindows(tone(440, 1), 0, 100), /hz must be in/u);
    assert.throws(() => goertzelWindows(tone(440, 1), 24_000, 100), /hz must be in/u);
    assert.throws(() => goertzelWindows(tone(440, 1), 440, 0), /windowMs is too small/u);
  });
});

describe("decodeMp4AudioMono on a real AAC track", () => {
  let directory;
  let tonePath;

  before(async () => {
    directory = await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), "tora-envelope-test-"));
    tonePath = join(directory, "tone.mp4");

    // 1 s of a 440 Hz sine at amplitude 0.5, encoded as AAC in an MP4 (what the renders produce).
    await runFfmpeg([
      "-v",
      "error",
      "-f",
      "lavfi",
      "-i",
      "sine=frequency=440:duration=1:sample_rate=48000",
      "-af",
      "volume=4,pan=stereo|c0=c0|c1=c0",
      "-c:a",
      "aac",
      "-b:a",
      "128k",
      "-f",
      "mp4",
      "-y",
      tonePath,
    ]);
  });
  after(async () => {
    await rm(directory, {recursive: true, force: true});
  });

  it("decodes about one second of mono 48 kHz samples that Goertzel finds at 440 Hz", async () => {
    const samples = await decodeMp4AudioMono(tonePath);

    assert.ok(samples instanceof Float32Array);
    assert.ok(samples.length >= 48_000 && samples.length <= 49_500, `${samples.length}`);

    const windows = goertzelWindows(samples, 440, 100);
    const middle = windows.slice(2, 8);

    for (const amplitude of middle) {
      near(amplitude, 0.5, 0.05);
    }

    // The positive control above proves the decode is not silence; now a wrong frequency.
    for (const amplitude of goertzelWindows(samples, 880, 100).slice(2, 8)) {
      assert.ok(amplitude < 0.03, `880 Hz read ${amplitude}`);
    }
  });

  it("throws a clear error for a file that cannot be decoded", async () => {
    await assert.rejects(
      decodeMp4AudioMono(join(directory, "missing.mp4")),
      /decodeMp4AudioMono failed/u,
    );
  });
});
```

Create `tests/helpers/audioEnvelope.mjs`:

```js
// Measuring the sound of a rendered MP4 (AUDIO-005 integration tests; AUDIO-006
// builds its parity and golden measurements on these two functions).
//
// The audio of the MP4 is decoded to mono PCM with the ffmpeg that ships with
// Remotion, then a Goertzel filter reads the amplitude of one frequency in
// consecutive windows. The fixtures are pure tones (music 220 Hz, voice 440 Hz,
// sfx 880 Hz), so the amplitude per window tells which track sounds and how loud.
// Always include a positive control: a broken decode returns silence that looks
// like legitimate silence.

import {mkdtemp, readFile, rm} from "node:fs/promises";
import {tmpdir} from "node:os";
import {join} from "node:path";
import {runFfmpeg} from "./ffmpeg.mjs";

export const ENVELOPE_SAMPLE_RATE = 48_000;

/** The `data` chunk of a 16-bit PCM WAV as floats in [-1, 1). */
const readPcm16Wav = (bytes) => {
  const buffer = Buffer.from(bytes.buffer, bytes.byteOffset, bytes.byteLength);
  let offset = 12;

  while (offset + 8 <= buffer.length) {
    const id = buffer.toString("ascii", offset, offset + 4);
    const size = buffer.readUInt32LE(offset + 4);

    if (id === "data") {
      const start = offset + 8;
      const end = Math.min(buffer.length, start + size);
      const samples = new Float32Array(Math.floor((end - start) / 2));

      for (let index = 0; index < samples.length; index += 1) {
        samples[index] = buffer.readInt16LE(start + index * 2) / 32768;
      }

      return samples;
    }

    offset += 8 + size + (size % 2);
  }

  throw new Error("The decoded WAV has no data chunk");
};

/**
 * The first audio stream of an MP4 (or any media file) decoded to mono floats at
 * 48 kHz with the repository ffmpeg. Throws when the file has no audio stream.
 */
export const decodeMp4AudioMono = async (path) => {
  const directory = await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), "tora-envelope-"));
  const output = join(directory, "audio.wav");

  try {
    try {
      await runFfmpeg([
        "-v",
        "error",
        "-i",
        path,
        "-map",
        "0:a:0",
        "-ac",
        "1",
        "-ar",
        String(ENVELOPE_SAMPLE_RATE),
        "-c:a",
        "pcm_s16le",
        "-y",
        output,
      ]);
    } catch (error) {
      throw new Error(
        `decodeMp4AudioMono failed on ${path}: ${error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)}`,
      );
    }

    return readPcm16Wav(new Uint8Array(await readFile(output)));
  } finally {
    await rm(directory, {recursive: true, force: true});
  }
};

/**
 * The amplitude of `hz` in consecutive, non-overlapping windows of `windowMs`
 * (a trailing partial window is dropped). A full-scale sine at `hz` reads ~1
 * and a sine of amplitude 0.25 reads ~0.25; silence and other frequencies read
 * ~0 (a window holds a whole number of cycles of the fixture tones at 100 ms).
 */
export const goertzelWindows = (
  samples,
  hz,
  windowMs,
  sampleRate = ENVELOPE_SAMPLE_RATE,
) => {
  const windowSize = Math.round((windowMs * sampleRate) / 1000);

  if (!Number.isFinite(hz) || hz <= 0 || hz >= sampleRate / 2) {
    throw new Error(`goertzelWindows: hz must be in (0, ${sampleRate / 2})`);
  }

  if (!Number.isInteger(windowSize) || windowSize < 1) {
    throw new Error("goertzelWindows: windowMs is too small");
  }

  const coefficient = 2 * Math.cos((2 * Math.PI * hz) / sampleRate);
  const amplitudes = [];

  for (
    let start = 0;
    start + windowSize <= samples.length;
    start += windowSize
  ) {
    let previous = 0;
    let beforePrevious = 0;

    for (let index = start; index < start + windowSize; index += 1) {
      const current = samples[index] + coefficient * previous - beforePrevious;

      beforePrevious = previous;
      previous = current;
    }

    const power =
      previous * previous +
      beforePrevious * beforePrevious -
      coefficient * previous * beforePrevious;

    amplitudes.push((2 * Math.sqrt(Math.max(0, power))) / windowSize);
  }

  return amplitudes;
};
```

Create `tests/helpers/fakeFfmpeg.mjs`:

```js
// A fake `child_process.spawn` for the CLI audio preflight (scripts/audioPreflight.ts):
// the tests decide what the "ffmpeg" writes and how it exits.

import {EventEmitter} from "node:events";
import {buildWav} from "./audioBytes.mjs";

export const FAKE_FFMPEG = {ffmpeg: "ffmpeg-under-test", dir: "dir-under-test"};

/**
 * A fake `spawn`. After the call, `script(child, call)` emits what the fake
 * ffmpeg does (`child.stdout` / `child.stderr` data, then `close`). Every call
 * is appended to `calls`; `calls.active` is the number of children that were
 * started and have not closed (a test can assert it never exceeds 1).
 */
export const fakeSpawn = (script, calls = []) => {
  calls.active = 0;
  calls.maxActive = 0;

  return (command, args, options) => {
    const child = new EventEmitter();
    const call = {command, args, options, child};

    child.stdout = new EventEmitter();
    child.stderr = new EventEmitter();
    child.killed = false;
    child.kill = () => {
      child.killed = true;
    };
    child.once("close", () => {
      calls.active -= 1;
    });
    calls.active += 1;
    calls.maxActive = Math.max(calls.maxActive, calls.active);
    calls.push(call);
    setImmediate(() => script(child, call));

    return child;
  };
};

/** A 48 kHz mono 16-bit WAV stream: a 44-byte header followed by `seconds` of silence. */
export const wavStream = (seconds) =>
  buildWav({sampleRate: 48_000, channels: 1, seconds});

/** The script of an ffmpeg that writes `bytes` in chunks and exits 0. */
export const succeedWith = (bytes, chunkSize = 4096) => (child) => {
  for (let offset = 0; offset < bytes.length; offset += chunkSize) {
    child.stdout.emit("data", bytes.subarray(offset, offset + chunkSize));
  }

  child.emit("close", 0, null);
};

/** The script of an ffmpeg that decodes `seconds` of audio and exits 0. */
export const decodeSeconds = (seconds) => succeedWith(wavStream(seconds));
```

```js
import assert from "node:assert/strict";
import {fileURLToPath} from "node:url";
import {describe, it} from "node:test";
import {
  AUDIO_PREFLIGHT_TIMEOUT_MS,
  checkAudioFileDecodes,
  createWavDataCounter,
  decodeAudioFileWithFfmpeg,
} from "../scripts/audioPreflight.ts";
import {inspectAudio} from "../src/localAssets/audioInspection.ts";
import {fixtureBytes} from "./helpers/audioBytes.mjs";
import {
  FAKE_FFMPEG as FFMPEG,
  fakeSpawn,
  succeedWith,
  wavStream,
} from "./helpers/fakeFfmpeg.mjs";

const fixturePath = (name) =>
  fileURLToPath(new URL(`./fixtures/local-audio/${name}`, import.meta.url));

describe("createWavDataCounter", () => {
  it("counts only the bytes after the data chunk header, whatever the chunk boundaries", () => {
    const wav = wavStream(1);

    for (const chunkSize of [1, 7, 44, 45, 1000, wav.length]) {
      const counter = createWavDataCounter();

      for (let offset = 0; offset < wav.length; offset += chunkSize) {
        counter.push(wav.subarray(offset, offset + chunkSize));
      }

      assert.equal(counter.dataBytes, 96_000, `chunk size ${chunkSize}`);
    }
  });

  it("skips extra header chunks such as LIST", () => {
    const base = wavStream(0.5);
    const list = Buffer.alloc(8 + 26);

    list.write("LIST", 0, "ascii");
    list.writeUInt32LE(26, 4);

    const wav = Buffer.concat([base.subarray(0, 36), list, base.subarray(36)]);
    const counter = createWavDataCounter();

    counter.push(wav);
    assert.equal(counter.dataBytes, 48_000);
  });

  it("rejects a stream that is not a WAV", () => {
    assert.throws(
      () => createWavDataCounter().push(Buffer.from("not a wav stream at all")),
      /did not write a WAV stream/u,
    );
  });
});

describe("decodeAudioFileWithFfmpeg with an injected spawn", () => {
  it("runs the repository ffmpeg on the first audio stream, mono 48 kHz, bounded, never reading stdin", async () => {
    const calls = [];
    const result = await decodeAudioFileWithFfmpeg("/staged/voice.wav", {
      spawn: fakeSpawn(succeedWith(wavStream(2.2)), calls),
      ffmpeg: FFMPEG,
    });

    assert.equal(result.ok, true);
    assert.ok(Math.abs(result.decodedSeconds - 2.2) < 1e-9);
    assert.equal(calls.length, 1);
    assert.equal(calls[0].command, "ffmpeg-under-test");
    assert.deepEqual(calls[0].args, [
      "-v",
      "error",
      "-nostdin",
      "-i",
      "file:/staged/voice.wav",
      "-map",
      "0:a:0",
      "-ac",
      "1",
      "-ar",
      "48000",
      "-t",
      "301",
      "-c:a",
      "pcm_s16le",
      "-f",
      "wav",
      "-",
    ]);
    assert.deepEqual(calls[0].options.stdio, ["ignore", "pipe", "pipe"]);
  });

  it("passes a path that starts with a dash as a file: input, never as an option", async () => {
    const calls = [];

    await decodeAudioFileWithFfmpeg("-stats.wav", {
      spawn: fakeSpawn(succeedWith(wavStream(0.1)), calls),
      ffmpeg: FFMPEG,
    });
    assert.equal(calls[0].args[calls[0].args.indexOf("-i") + 1], "file:-stats.wav");
  });

  it("attaches the child to the interrupt guard before it can produce anything", async () => {
    const attached = [];
    const calls = [];

    await decodeAudioFileWithFfmpeg("/staged/a.wav", {
      spawn: fakeSpawn(succeedWith(wavStream(0.1)), calls),
      ffmpeg: FFMPEG,
      control: {attach: (child) => attached.push(child)},
    });
    assert.equal(attached.length, 1);
    assert.equal(attached[0], calls[0].child);
  });

  it("stops ffmpeg that decodes more than the bound (a container that lies about its length)", async () => {
    const calls = [];
    const result = await decodeAudioFileWithFfmpeg("/staged/liar.mp3", {
      spawn: fakeSpawn((child) => {
        const header = wavStream(0).subarray(0, 44);
        const second = Buffer.alloc(96_000);

        child.stdout.emit("data", header);

        // 303 s is past the bound of 301 s plus one second of slack.
        for (let index = 0; index < 304 && !child.killed; index += 1) {
          child.stdout.emit("data", second);
        }
      }, calls),
      ffmpeg: FFMPEG,
    });

    assert.deepEqual([result.ok, result.reason], [false, "output"]);
    assert.match(result.detail, /more than 301 s/u);
    assert.equal(calls[0].child.killed, true);
  });

  it("fails on a non-zero exit and keeps the first lines of stderr", async () => {
    const result = await decodeAudioFileWithFfmpeg("/staged/a.mp3", {
      spawn: fakeSpawn((child) => {
        child.stderr.emit("data", Buffer.from("Invalid data found when processing input"));
        child.emit("close", 1, null);
      }),
      ffmpeg: FFMPEG,
    });

    assert.equal(result.ok, false);
    assert.equal(result.reason, "exit");
    assert.match(result.detail, /exited with code 1: Invalid data found/u);
  });

  it("kills ffmpeg and fails when it does not finish within the timeout", async () => {
    const calls = [];
    const result = await decodeAudioFileWithFfmpeg("/staged/a.mp3", {
      spawn: fakeSpawn(() => undefined, calls),
      ffmpeg: FFMPEG,
      timeoutMs: 20,
    });

    assert.equal(result.ok, false);
    assert.equal(result.reason, "timeout");
    assert.equal(calls[0].child.killed, true);
    assert.equal(AUDIO_PREFLIGHT_TIMEOUT_MS, 60_000);
  });

  it("fails when ffmpeg cannot be started, whether spawn throws or emits an error", async () => {
    const thrown = await decodeAudioFileWithFfmpeg("/a", {
      spawn: () => {
        throw new Error("ENOENT");
      },
      ffmpeg: FFMPEG,
    });
    const emitted = await decodeAudioFileWithFfmpeg("/a", {
      spawn: fakeSpawn((child) => child.emit("error", new Error("spawn EACCES"))),
      ffmpeg: FFMPEG,
    });

    assert.deepEqual([thrown.ok, thrown.reason], [false, "spawn"]);
    assert.deepEqual([emitted.ok, emitted.reason], [false, "spawn"]);
  });

  it("fails when the output is not a WAV stream, and stops ffmpeg", async () => {
    const calls = [];
    const result = await decodeAudioFileWithFfmpeg("/a", {
      spawn: fakeSpawn(
        (child) => child.stdout.emit("data", Buffer.from("garbage garbage garbage")),
        calls,
      ),
      ffmpeg: FFMPEG,
    });

    assert.deepEqual([result.ok, result.reason], [false, "output"]);
    assert.equal(calls[0].child.killed, true);
  });
});

describe("checkAudioFileDecodes applies the D-9 truncation rule", () => {
  const check = (decodedSeconds, durationSeconds) =>
    checkAudioFileDecodes("/staged/a.wav", durationSeconds, {
      spawn: fakeSpawn(succeedWith(wavStream(decodedSeconds))),
      ffmpeg: FFMPEG,
    });

  it("passes a decode 0.2 s short and refuses one 0.3 s short of the inspected duration", async () => {
    assert.equal((await check(9.8, 10)).ok, true);

    const short = await check(9.7, 10);

    assert.equal(short.ok, false);
    assert.match(short.detail, /only 9\.70 s of 10\.00 s could be decoded/u);
  });

  it("refuses exit 0 with zero decoded bytes, which is what a 0.12 s clip that decodes to nothing looks like", async () => {
    const result = await checkAudioFileDecodes("/staged/pop.wav", 0.12, {
      spawn: fakeSpawn(succeedWith(wavStream(0))),
      ffmpeg: FFMPEG,
    });

    assert.equal(result.ok, false);
    assert.match(result.detail, /only 0\.00 s of 0\.12 s/u);
  });

  it("passes a non-zero decode of a short clip and passes the clip when everything decodes", async () => {
    assert.equal((await check(0.11, 0.12)).ok, true);
    assert.equal((await check(0.12, 0.12)).ok, true);
  });

  it("returns the process failure as the detail", async () => {
    const result = await checkAudioFileDecodes("/staged/a.mp3", 5, {
      spawn: fakeSpawn((child) => child.emit("close", 2, null)),
      ffmpeg: FFMPEG,
    });

    assert.equal(result.ok, false);
    assert.match(result.detail, /exited with code 2/u);
  });
});

describe("checkAudioFileDecodes with the real ffmpeg on the fixtures", () => {
  const durationOf = async (name) => {
    const inspected = await inspectAudio(fixtureBytes(name));

    assert.equal(inspected.ok, true, name);

    assert.equal(result.ok, false);
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the implementation**

`scripts/ffmpegBinary.mjs` is plain JavaScript on purpose (a `.ts` file cannot be imported by the `.mjs` helpers that Playwright loads); `ffmpegBinary.d.mts` types it for `tsc`.

Create `scripts/ffmpegBinary.mjs`:

```js
// The ffmpeg and ffprobe binaries that ship in the installed
// @remotion/compositor-* package. No `npx` (not spawnable without a shell on
// Windows) and no system ffmpeg: the CLI audio preflight and the test helpers
// call exactly these binaries, so Windows, macOS and Linux behave alike.
//
// Plain JavaScript on purpose (types: ffmpegBinary.d.mts): Playwright's loader
// cannot import a .ts file from the .mjs test helpers that also use this.

import {chmodSync, statSync} from "node:fs";
import {createRequire} from "node:module";
import {dirname, join} from "node:path";
import process from "node:process";

const require = createRequire(import.meta.url);

/** True when this Linux uses musl rather than glibc (the compositor ships both). */
const detectMusl = () => {
  const report =
    typeof process.report?.getReport === "function"
      ? process.report.getReport()
      : null;

  return report !== null && typeof report === "object"
    ? !report.header?.glibcVersionRuntime
    : false;
};

/**
 * Name of the npm package that holds ffmpeg/ffprobe for a platform, e.g.
 * `@remotion/compositor-win32-x64-msvc` or `@remotion/compositor-linux-x64-gnu`.
 */
export const compositorPackageName = ({
  platform = process.platform,
  arch = process.arch,
  musl,
} = {}) => {
  switch (platform) {
    case "win32":
      if (arch === "x64") {
        return "@remotion/compositor-win32-x64-msvc";
      }

      break;
    case "darwin":
      if (arch === "x64" || arch === "arm64") {
        return `@remotion/compositor-darwin-${arch}`;
      }

      break;
    case "linux":
      if (arch === "x64" || arch === "arm64") {
        const libc = (musl ?? detectMusl()) ? "musl" : "gnu";

        return `@remotion/compositor-linux-${arch}-${libc}`;
      }

      break;
    default:
      throw new Error(
        `Unsupported platform ${platform} (architecture ${arch}) for the Remotion ffmpeg binaries`,
      );
  }

  throw new Error(
    `Unsupported architecture ${arch} on ${platform} for the Remotion ffmpeg binaries`,
  );
};

const executableName = (name, platform) =>
  platform === "win32" ? `${name}.exe` : name;

const ensureExecutable = (path) => {
  if (process.platform === "win32") {
    return;
  }

  try {
    if ((statSync(path).mode & 0o111) === 0) {
      chmodSync(path, 0o755);
    }
  } catch {
    // Let the spawn report the real problem.
  }
};

let cachedBinaries = null;

/**
 * `{dir, ffmpeg, ffprobe}`: absolute paths from the installed compositor
 * package. `resolvePackage` replaces module resolution (tests).
 */
export const resolveFfmpegBinaries = ({
  platform = process.platform,
  arch = process.arch,
  musl,
  resolvePackage = (name) => require.resolve(`${name}/package.json`),
} = {}) => {
  const useCache = platform === process.platform && arch === process.arch;

  if (useCache && musl === undefined && cachedBinaries !== null) {
    return cachedBinaries;
  }

  const packageName = compositorPackageName({platform, arch, musl});
  let packageJson;

  try {
    packageJson = resolvePackage(packageName);
  } catch (error) {
    throw new Error(
      `Could not find ${packageName}, which provides ffmpeg for ${platform}/${arch}. Run npm install. (${error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)})`,
    );
  }

  const dir = dirname(packageJson);
  const binaries = {
    dir,
    ffmpeg: join(dir, executableName("ffmpeg", platform)),
    ffprobe: join(dir, executableName("ffprobe", platform)),
  };

  if (useCache && musl === undefined) {
    ensureExecutable(binaries.ffmpeg);
    ensureExecutable(binaries.ffprobe);
    cachedBinaries = binaries;
  }

  return binaries;
};
```

Create `scripts/ffmpegBinary.d.mts`:

```
export type FfmpegBinaries = {dir: string; ffmpeg: string; ffprobe: string};

/**
 * Name of the npm package that holds ffmpeg/ffprobe for a platform, e.g.
 * `@remotion/compositor-win32-x64-msvc` or `@remotion/compositor-linux-x64-gnu`.
 */
export function compositorPackageName(options?: {
  platform?: string;
  arch?: string;
  musl?: boolean;
}): string;

/**
 * `{dir, ffmpeg, ffprobe}`: absolute paths from the installed compositor
 * package. `resolvePackage` replaces module resolution (tests).
 */
export function resolveFfmpegBinaries(options?: {
  platform?: string;
  arch?: string;
  musl?: boolean;
  resolvePackage?: (packageName: string) => string;
}): FfmpegBinaries;
```

```js
import assert from "node:assert/strict";

const require = createRequire(import.meta.url);

const PROCESS_TIMEOUT_MS = 60_000;
const MAX_BUFFER_BYTES = 32 * 1024 * 1024;

/** True when this Linux uses musl rather than glibc (the compositor ships both). */
const detectMusl = () => {
  const report =
    typeof process.report?.getReport === "function"
      ? process.report.getReport()
      : null;

  return report !== null && typeof report === "object"
    ? !report.header?.glibcVersionRuntime
    : false;
};

/**
 * Name of the npm package that holds ffmpeg/ffprobe for a platform, e.g.
 * `@remotion/compositor-win32-x64-msvc` or `@remotion/compositor-linux-x64-gnu`.
 */
export const compositorPackageName = ({
  platform = process.platform,
  arch = process.arch,
  musl,
} = {}) => {
  switch (platform) {
    case "win32":
      if (arch === "x64") {
        return "@remotion/compositor-win32-x64-msvc";
      }

      break;
    case "darwin":
      if (arch === "x64" || arch === "arm64") {
        return `@remotion/compositor-darwin-${arch}`;

    case "linux":

        return `@remotion/compositor-linux-${arch}-${libc}`;
      }

      break;
    default:
      throw new Error(
        `Unsupported platform ${platform} (architecture ${arch}) for the Remotion ffmpeg binaries`,
      );
  }

  throw new Error(
    `Unsupported architecture ${arch} on ${platform} for the Remotion ffmpeg binaries`,
  );
};

const executableName = (name, platform) =>
  platform === "win32" ? `${name}.exe` : name;

const ensureExecutable = (path) => {
  if (process.platform === "win32") {
    return;
  }

  try {
    if ((statSync(path).mode & 0o111) === 0) {
      chmodSync(path, 0o755);
    }
  } catch {
    // Let the spawn report the real problem.
  }
};

let cachedBinaries = null;

/**
 * `{dir, ffmpeg, ffprobe}`: absolute paths from the installed compositor
 * package. `resolvePackage` replaces module resolution (tests).
 */
export const resolveFfmpegBinaries = ({
  platform = process.platform,
  arch = process.arch,
  musl,
  resolvePackage = (name) => require.resolve(`${name}/package.json`),
} = {}) => {
  const useCache = platform === process.platform && arch === process.arch;

  if (useCache && musl === undefined && cachedBinaries !== null) {
    return cachedBinaries;
  }

  const packageName = compositorPackageName({platform, arch, musl});
  let packageJson;

  try {
    packageJson = resolvePackage(packageName);
  } catch (error) {
    throw new Error(
      `Could not find ${packageName}, which provides ffmpeg for ${platform}/${arch}. Run npm install. (${error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)})`,
    );
  }

  const dir = dirname(packageJson);
  const binaries = {
    dir,
    ffmpeg: join(dir, executableName("ffmpeg", platform)),
    ffprobe: join(dir, executableName("ffprobe", platform)),
  };

  if (useCache && musl === undefined) {
    ensureExecutable(binaries.ffmpeg);
    ensureExecutable(binaries.ffprobe);
    cachedBinaries = binaries;
  }

  return binaries;
};

const run = async (binary, dir, args, options = {}) => {
  const libraryPath = [dir, process.env.LD_LIBRARY_PATH]
    .filter(Boolean)
```

Create `scripts/audioPreflight.ts`:

```ts
// CLI decode preflight (AINV-8): before Remotion is spawned, every staged audio
// file the render will read is decoded once with the ffmpeg that ships with
// Remotion, and the decoded length must satisfy the D-9 truncation rule.
// Node has no WebCodecs, but the decoder here is ffmpeg, the same family
// Remotion's own audio extraction uses.
//
// The repository ffmpeg is a minimal build: it has the `wav` muxer but no raw
// `f32le`/`s16le` one, so the file is decoded to a mono 48 kHz 16-bit WAV on
// stdout and only the bytes after the WAV header are counted (nothing is buffered).
//
// The preflight fails on a non-zero exit, on a decode that produced nothing, on
// output past the bound below, or on the D-9 truncation rule; it does not use
// `-xerror`, so a file the browser import accepts (the same tolerance) is
// accepted here too. The staged path is digest- or id-named and absolute, is
// passed with a `file:` prefix (it can never parse as an option or a protocol)
// and ffmpeg never reads stdin.

import {spawn as nodeSpawn} from "node:child_process";
import {isDecodeComplete} from "../src/audio/decodeCheck.ts";
import {MAX_AUDIO_DURATION_SECONDS} from "../src/localAssets/limits.ts";
import {resolveFfmpegBinaries} from "./ffmpegBinary.mjs";

export const AUDIO_PREFLIGHT_TIMEOUT_MS = 60_000;
export const PREFLIGHT_SAMPLE_RATE = 48_000;
/** 16-bit mono. */
const PREFLIGHT_BYTES_PER_SECOND = PREFLIGHT_SAMPLE_RATE * 2;
/** AINV-7 bounds every audio source at 300 s; a file whose container lies about its length stops here. */
export const PREFLIGHT_MAX_SECONDS = MAX_AUDIO_DURATION_SECONDS + 1;
/** Decoded bytes past this (the cap plus a second of slack) stop the child: nothing is ever buffered. */
export const PREFLIGHT_MAX_DECODED_BYTES =
  (PREFLIGHT_MAX_SECONDS + 1) * PREFLIGHT_BYTES_PER_SECOND;
/** A WAV header written by ffmpeg is ~80 bytes; anything this long is not a header. */
const MAX_WAV_HEADER_BYTES = 64 * 1024;
const MAX_STDERR_CHARS = 2_000;

/** The part of a child process the preflight uses (`child_process.spawn` satisfies it). */
export type PreflightChild = {
  stdout: {on(event: "data", listener: (chunk: Uint8Array) => void): unknown} | null;
  stderr: {on(event: "data", listener: (chunk: Uint8Array) => void): unknown} | null;
  /** Present for a real child; the interrupt guard signals its process tree through it. */
  pid?: number;
  on(event: "error", listener: (error: Error) => void): unknown;
  on(
    event: "close",
    listener: (code: number | null, signal: string | null) => void,
  ): unknown;
  once(event: "close", listener: () => void): unknown;
  off(event: "close", listener: () => void): unknown;
  kill(signal?: NodeJS.Signals): boolean;
};

export type PreflightSpawn = (
  command: string,
  args: string[],
  options: {
    stdio: ["ignore", "pipe", "pipe"];
    windowsHide: true;
    env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv;
  },
) => PreflightChild;

export type AudioPreflightDeps = {
  /** Replaces `child_process.spawn` (tests). */
  spawn?: PreflightSpawn;
  /** Replaces the ffmpeg lookup (tests). */
  ffmpeg?: {ffmpeg: string; dir: string};
  timeoutMs?: number;
  /**
   * The interrupt guard of the command (`RenderControl`): the ffmpeg child is
   * attached to it, so Ctrl+C and SIGTERM stop the decode instead of waiting for it.
   */
  control?: {attach(child: PreflightChild): void};
};

export type FfmpegDecodeResult =
  | {ok: true; decodedSeconds: number}
  | {ok: false; reason: "exit" | "timeout" | "spawn" | "output"; detail: string};

export type AudioDecodeCheck =
  | {ok: true; decodedSeconds: number}
  | {ok: false; detail: string};

const concatBytes = (left: Uint8Array, right: Uint8Array): Uint8Array => {
  const joined = new Uint8Array(left.length + right.length);

  joined.set(left, 0);
  joined.set(right, left.length);

  return joined;
};

/** Index of the first PCM byte once the WAV header is complete, -1 while more bytes are needed. */
const findWavDataStart = (header: Uint8Array): number => {
  if (header.length < 12) {
    return -1;
  }

  const text = (start: number, end: number) =>
    String.fromCharCode(...header.subarray(start, end));

  if (text(0, 4) !== "RIFF" || text(8, 12) !== "WAVE") {
    throw new Error("ffmpeg did not write a WAV stream");
  }

  const view = new DataView(header.buffer, header.byteOffset, header.byteLength);
  let offset = 12;

  while (offset + 8 <= header.length) {
    const size = view.getUint32(offset + 4, true);

    if (text(offset, offset + 4) === "data") {
      return offset + 8;
    }

    offset += 8 + size + (size % 2);
  }

  return -1;
};

/** Counts the PCM bytes of a streamed WAV without keeping them. */
export const createWavDataCounter = () => {
  let header: Uint8Array = new Uint8Array(0);
  let headerDone = false;
  let dataBytes = 0;

  return {
    push(chunk: Uint8Array): void {
      if (headerDone) {
        dataBytes += chunk.length;

        return;
      }

      header = concatBytes(header, chunk);

      const start = findWavDataStart(header);

      if (start >= 0) {
        headerDone = true;
        dataBytes = header.length - start;
        header = new Uint8Array(0);
      } else if (header.length > MAX_WAV_HEADER_BYTES) {
        throw new Error("ffmpeg did not write a WAV header");
      }
    },
    get headerComplete(): boolean {
      return headerDone;
    },
    get dataBytes(): number {
      return dataBytes;
    },
  };
};

/**
 * Decodes the first audio stream of `path` to mono 48 kHz PCM with the
 * repository ffmpeg and reports how many seconds came out. Any non-zero exit, a
 * spawn failure, too much output or `timeoutMs` without an exit is a failure.
 */
export const decodeAudioFileWithFfmpeg = (
  path: string,
  deps: AudioPreflightDeps = {},
): Promise<FfmpegDecodeResult> =>
  new Promise((resolve) => {
    const binaries = deps.ffmpeg ?? resolveFfmpegBinaries();
    const spawnProcess = deps.spawn ?? (nodeSpawn as unknown as PreflightSpawn);
    const timeoutMs = deps.timeoutMs ?? AUDIO_PREFLIGHT_TIMEOUT_MS;
    const counter = createWavDataCounter();
    let stderr = "";
    let settled = false;
    let timer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | null = null;
    let child: PreflightChild | null = null;

    const finish = (result: FfmpegDecodeResult) => {
      if (settled) {
        return;
      }

      settled = true;

      if (timer !== null) {
        clearTimeout(timer);
      }

      resolve(result);
    };
    const kill = () => {
      try {
        child?.kill();
      } catch {
        // The process already exited.
      }
    };

    try {
      child = spawnProcess(
        binaries.ffmpeg,
        [
          "-v",
          "error",
          "-nostdin",
          "-i",
          `file:${path}`,
          "-map",
          "0:a:0",
          "-ac",
          "1",
          "-ar",
          String(PREFLIGHT_SAMPLE_RATE),
          "-t",
          String(PREFLIGHT_MAX_SECONDS),
          "-c:a",
          "pcm_s16le",
          "-f",
          "wav",
          "-",
        ],
        {
          stdio: ["ignore", "pipe", "pipe"],
          windowsHide: true,
          env: {
            ...process.env,
            LD_LIBRARY_PATH: [binaries.dir, process.env.LD_LIBRARY_PATH]
              .filter(Boolean)
              .join(":"),
            DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH: [binaries.dir, process.env.DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH]
              .filter(Boolean)
              .join(":"),
          },
        },
      );
    } catch (error) {
      finish({
        ok: false,
        reason: "spawn",
        detail: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error),
      });

      return;
    }

    deps.control?.attach(child);

    timer = setTimeout(() => {
      kill();
      finish({
        ok: false,
        reason: "timeout",
        detail: `ffmpeg did not finish within ${timeoutMs / 1000} s`,
      });
    }, timeoutMs);

    child.stdout?.on("data", (chunk) => {
      try {
        counter.push(chunk);

        if (counter.dataBytes > PREFLIGHT_MAX_DECODED_BYTES) {
          kill();
          finish({
            ok: false,
            reason: "output",
            detail: `ffmpeg decoded more than ${PREFLIGHT_MAX_SECONDS} s of audio`,
          });
        }
      } catch (error) {
        kill();
        finish({
          ok: false,
          reason: "output",
          detail: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error),
        });
      }
    });
    child.stderr?.on("data", (chunk) => {
      if (stderr.length < MAX_STDERR_CHARS) {
        stderr += Buffer.from(chunk).toString("utf8");
      }
    });
    child.on("error", (error) => {
      finish({ok: false, reason: "spawn", detail: error.message});
    });
    child.on("close", (code, signal) => {
      if (code !== 0) {
        const how =
          signal === null
            ? `exited with code ${code ?? "unknown"}`
            : `was stopped by ${signal}`;
        const why = stderr.trim() === "" ? "" : `: ${stderr.trim().slice(0, MAX_STDERR_CHARS)}`;

        finish({ok: false, reason: "exit", detail: `ffmpeg ${how}${why}`});

        return;
      }

      finish({
        ok: true,
        decodedSeconds: counter.dataBytes / PREFLIGHT_BYTES_PER_SECOND,
      });
    });
  });

/**
 * The preflight of one staged file: it decodes completely (D-9) or the
 * detail says why not. `durationSeconds` is the `inspectAudio` value that the
 * timeline uses for this file (AINV-3).
 */
export const checkAudioFileDecodes = async (
  path: string,
  durationSeconds: number,
  deps: AudioPreflightDeps = {},
): Promise<AudioDecodeCheck> => {
  const decoded = await decodeAudioFileWithFfmpeg(path, deps);

  if (!decoded.ok) {
    return {ok: false, detail: decoded.detail};
  }

  if (!isDecodeComplete(decoded.decodedSeconds, durationSeconds)) {
    return {
      ok: false,
      detail: `only ${decoded.decodedSeconds.toFixed(2)} s of ${durationSeconds.toFixed(2)} s could be decoded`,
    };
  }

  return {ok: true, decodedSeconds: decoded.decodedSeconds};
};
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the implementation**

Create `src/audio/readiness.ts`:

```ts
import type {Story} from "../story/types.ts";
import type {AudioRef} from "./catalog.ts";
import type {AudioRole} from "./plan.ts";

/** One use of an audio ref by the Story: the music, or the sfx / voice of a scene. */
export type StoryAudioRef = {
  ref: AudioRef;
  role: AudioRole;
  /** 0-based; null for the music. */
  sceneIndex: number | null;
};

/**
 * Every use of an audio ref in the order a person reads the Story: the music,
 * then each scene's sfx and voice. A ref used twice appears twice (once per
 * role), unlike `storyAudioRefs`, which lists distinct refs.
 */
export const collectStoryAudioRefs = (story: Story): StoryAudioRef[] => {
  const uses: StoryAudioRef[] = [];

  if (story.music !== undefined) {
    uses.push({ref: story.music.src, role: "music", sceneIndex: null});
  }

  for (const [sceneIndex, scene] of story.scenes.entries()) {
    if (scene.sfx !== undefined) {
      uses.push({ref: scene.sfx.src, role: "sfx", sceneIndex});
    }

    if (scene.voice !== undefined) {
      uses.push({ref: scene.voice.src, role: "voice", sceneIndex});
    }
  }

  return uses;
};

/** `music`, `scene 3 sfx`, `scene 3 voice` (scenes are 1-based in text). */
export const describeAudioRole = (
  role: AudioRole,
  sceneIndex: number | null,
): string =>
  sceneIndex === null || role === "music"
    ? role
    : `scene ${sceneIndex + 1} ${role}`;

/** The roles in which `ref` is used, as `describeAudioRole` text, in Story order. */
export const describeAudioRefRoles = (story: Story, ref: AudioRef): string[] =>
  collectStoryAudioRefs(story)
    .filter((use) => use.ref === ref)
    .map((use) => describeAudioRole(use.role, use.sceneIndex));

export const bundledAudioUnavailableMessage = (id: string): string =>
  `Bundled audio ${id} is not available in this build.`;

export const bundledAudioMismatchMessage = (id: string): string =>
  `Bundled audio ${id} does not match the catalog.`;

/** The one text for a source that failed a render preflight (metadata or decode). */
export const audioFileUnusableMessage = (name: string): string =>
  `Audio file ${name} cannot be used for rendering.`;
```

### Task 4: CLI audio staging, measuring, the plan check and the decode preflight

**Files:**
- Create: `scripts/audioRender.ts`, `tests/cli-audio-render.test.mjs`
- Modify: `scripts/localAssets.ts` (`onStaged` gets the file's path inside its category folder), `tests/cli-local-assets.test.mjs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 2 (`checkAudioFileDecodes`, `AudioPreflightDeps`, `tests/helpers/fakeFfmpeg.mjs`), Task 3 (`describeAudioRefRoles`, `describeAudioRole`, the messages), `stageLocalAssetsForStory` (AUDIO-003: it already stages `local:audio:` refs from `local-assets/audio/`), `inspectAudio`, `storyAudioDurations`, `buildAudioSourceMap`, `compileAudioPlan`, `resolveAudioSrc`.
- Produces:
  - `scripts/localAssets.ts`: `stageLocalAssetsForStory(..., {onStaged?: (ref, stagedPath, relativePath) => ...})` (third argument added; existing callers ignore it).
  - `scripts/audioRender.ts`: `STAGED_BUNDLED_AUDIO_DIR = "__local-assets/bundled"`; `class AudioRenderError extends Error`; `prepareCliAudio(story, options: {publicDir: string; localSources: LocalAssetSourceMap; localNames?: ReadonlyMap<string, string>; publicSourceDir?: string; checkInterrupt?: () => void; control?: {attach(child)}; preflight?: AudioPreflightDeps}): Promise<{audioDurations: AudioDurationMap; audioSources: AudioSourceMap}>`. It stages every bundled file under `<publicDir>/__local-assets/bundled/<id>.<ext>` (a catalog `file: null` or a file missing from `<publicSourceDir>` becomes `{kind: "unavailable"}`), runs `inspectAudio` on each staged file once (a local file's duration is the one the timeline uses, AINV-3; a bundled file must match its catalog duration within 1 ms), compiles the plan with `bundledAudioPolicy: "explicit"` semantics and refuses on `missing-audio`, `pending-audio` or `story-too-long-for-audio`, and decodes each staged file once, one at a time. Every refusal is an `AudioRenderError`.

```js
import assert from "node:assert/strict";
import {createHash} from "node:crypto";
import {cp, mkdir, mkdtemp, readFile, readdir, rm, writeFile} from "node:fs/promises";
import {tmpdir} from "node:os";
import {join} from "node:path";
import {after, before, describe, it} from "node:test";
import {
  AudioRenderError,
  prepareCliAudio,
  STAGED_BUNDLED_AUDIO_DIR,
} from "../scripts/audioRender.ts";
import {stageLocalAssetsForStory} from "../scripts/localAssets.ts";
import {buildWav, fixtureBytes} from "./helpers/audioBytes.mjs";
import {
  decodeSeconds,
  FAKE_FFMPEG,
  fakeSpawn,
} from "./helpers/fakeFfmpeg.mjs";

const sha = (bytes) => createHash("sha256").update(bytes).digest("hex");
const scene = (overrides = {}) => ({
  type: "dialogue",
  pose: "formal",
  background: "office",
  text: "Hi",
  duration: 3,
  ...overrides,
});
const storyOf = (...scenes) => ({title: "Audio", scenes});
const directories = [];
const tempDirectory = async () => {
  const directory = await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), "tora-cli-audio-"));

  directories.push(directory);

  return directory;
};

after(async () => {
  for (const directory of directories) {
    await rm(directory, {recursive: true, force: true});
  }
});

describe("prepareCliAudio", () => {
  let publicSource;

  /** A copy of the repository's bundled sfx, so a test can change a "bundled" file safely. */
  before(async () => {
    publicSource = await tempDirectory();
    await cp("public/audio", join(publicSource, "audio"), {recursive: true});
  });

  const prepare = async (story, options = {}) => {
    const publicDir = options.publicDir ?? (await tempDirectory());
    const names = new Map();
    let localSources = {};

    if (options.localRoot !== undefined) {
      const staged = await stageLocalAssetsForStory(story, {
        root: options.localRoot,
        publicDir,
        onStaged: (ref, _stagedPath, relativePath) => names.set(ref, relativePath),
      });

      localSources = staged.sources;
    }

    return {
      publicDir,
      names,
      result: await prepareCliAudio(story, {
        publicDir,
        localSources,
        localNames: names,
        publicSourceDir: options.publicSourceDir ?? publicSource,
        checkInterrupt: options.checkInterrupt,
        control: options.control,
        preflight: options.preflight,
      }),
    };
  };
  const refuses = async (story, options, pattern) => {
    let error = null;

    try {
      await prepare(story, options);
    } catch (caught) {
      error = caught;
    }

    assert.ok(error instanceof AudioRenderError, String(error));
    assert.match(error.message, pattern);

    return error;
  };
  const fakePreflight = (script, calls, timeoutMs) => ({
    spawn: fakeSpawn(script, calls),
    ffmpeg: FAKE_FFMPEG,
    ...(timeoutMs === undefined ? {} : {timeoutMs}),
  });

  it("stages a bundled sfx under __local-assets/bundled and points the sources at the staged copy", async () => {
    const story = storyOf(scene({sfx: {src: "ding"}}));
    const {publicDir, result} = await prepare(story);
    const original = await readFile(join(publicSource, "audio", "sfx", "ding.wav"));

    assert.deepEqual(result.audioSources, {
      ding: {kind: "static", path: `${STAGED_BUNDLED_AUDIO_DIR}/ding.wav`},
    });
    assert.equal(STAGED_BUNDLED_AUDIO_DIR, "__local-assets/bundled");
    assert.deepEqual(result.audioDurations, {ding: 1.2});
    assert.deepEqual(
      await readFile(join(publicDir, "__local-assets", "bundled", "ding.wav")),
      original,
    );
  });

  it("measures a staged local voice (AINV-3) and stages local and bundled sources together", async () => {
    const root = await tempDirectory();
    const voice = fixtureBytes("tone-440-2.2s.wav");
    const voiceRef = `local:audio:sha256:${sha(voice)}`;

    await mkdir(join(root, "audio"), {recursive: true});
    await writeFile(join(root, "audio", "voice.wav"), voice);

    const story = storyOf(
      scene({voice: {src: voiceRef}, sfx: {src: "ding"}, duration: "auto"}),
    );
    const {result} = await prepare(story, {localRoot: root});

    assert.deepEqual(result.audioDurations, {[voiceRef]: 2.2, ding: 1.2});
    assert.deepEqual(result.audioSources, {
      [voiceRef]: {kind: "static", path: `__local-assets/audio/${sha(voice)}.wav`},
      ding: {kind: "static", path: "__local-assets/bundled/ding.wav"},
    });
  });

  it("refuses a bundled id whose catalog file is null before anything is spawned, naming the role", async () => {
    const calls = [];

    await refuses(
      {...storyOf(scene({sfx: {src: "ding"}})), music: {src: "calm-loop"}},
      {preflight: fakePreflight(decodeSeconds(1.2), calls)},
      /^Cannot render Story: some of its audio is not available\.\n\n {2}music: Bundled audio calm-loop is not available in this build\.$/u,
    );
    assert.equal(calls.length, 0);
  });

  it("refuses a bundled file that is absent from public/audio and lists every problem with its role", async () => {
    const source = await tempDirectory();
    const calls = [];

    await cp("public/audio", join(source, "audio"), {recursive: true});
    await rm(join(source, "audio", "sfx", "whoosh.wav"));

    const error = await refuses(
      {
        ...storyOf(scene({sfx: {src: "ding"}}), scene({sfx: {src: "whoosh"}})),
        music: {src: "upbeat-loop"},
      },
      {publicSourceDir: source, preflight: fakePreflight(decodeSeconds(1.2), calls)},
      /scene 2 sfx: Bundled audio whoosh is not available in this build\./u,
    );

    assert.match(error.message, /music: Bundled audio upbeat-loop is not available in this build\./u);
    assert.doesNotMatch(error.message, /ding/u);
    assert.equal(calls.length, 0);
  });

  it("refuses a Story longer than 3,600 s with audio before spawning", async () => {
    const calls = [];

    await refuses(
      storyOf(scene({duration: 3_601, sfx: {src: "ding"}})),
      {preflight: fakePreflight(decodeSeconds(1.2), calls)},
      /Stories with audio/u,
    );
    assert.equal(calls.length, 0);
  });

  it("refuses a bundled file replaced by an MP4 with a video track in the metadata step, before any decode", async () => {
    const source = await tempDirectory();
    const calls = [];

    await cp("public/audio", join(source, "audio"), {recursive: true});
    await writeFile(join(source, "audio", "sfx", "ding.wav"), fixtureBytes("with-video.mp4"));

    const error = await refuses(
      storyOf(scene({sfx: {src: "ding"}})),
      {publicSourceDir: source, preflight: fakePreflight(decodeSeconds(1.2), calls)},
      /Audio file ding cannot be used for rendering\./u,
    );

    assert.match(error.message, /used by: scene 1 sfx/u);
    assert.match(error.message, /Video files are not supported/u);
    assert.equal(calls.length, 0);
  });

  it("refuses a bundled WAV whose duration differs from the catalog", async () => {
    const source = await tempDirectory();
    const calls = [];

    await cp("public/audio", join(source, "audio"), {recursive: true});
    await writeFile(join(source, "audio", "sfx", "ding.wav"), buildWav({seconds: 5}));

    await refuses(
      storyOf(scene({sfx: {src: "ding"}})),
      {publicSourceDir: source, preflight: fakePreflight(decodeSeconds(1.2), calls)},
      /^Bundled audio ding does not match the catalog\.$/u,
    );
    assert.equal(calls.length, 0);
  });

  it("decodes every staged file once with ffmpeg: a decode 0.2 s short passes, 0.3 s short, a non-zero exit and a timeout refuse", async () => {
    const story = storyOf(scene({sfx: {src: "ding"}}));
    const passCalls = [];

    // ding is 1.2 s: 1.0 s decoded is 0.2 s short and passes (tolerance 0.25 s).
    await prepare(story, { preflight: fakePreflight(decodeSeconds(1.0), passCalls) });
    assert.equal(passCalls.length, 1);
    assert.match(passCalls[0].args.join(" "), /__local-assets[\\/]bundled[\\/]ding\.wav/u);

    await refuses(
      story,
      {preflight: fakePreflight(decodeSeconds(0.9), [])},
      /only 0\.90 s of 1\.20 s could be decoded/u,
    );
    await refuses(
      story,
      {
        preflight: fakePreflight((child) => {
          child.stderr.emit("data", Buffer.from("Invalid data found when processing input"));
          child.emit("close", 1, null);
        }, []),
      },
      /Audio file ding cannot be used for rendering\.[\s\S]*exited with code 1: Invalid data found/u,
    );
    await refuses(
      story,
      {preflight: fakePreflight(() => undefined, [], 20)},
      /did not finish within/u,
    );
  });

  it("decodes a shared source once and one file at a time", async () => {
    const calls = [];

    await prepare(
      storyOf(
        scene({sfx: {src: "ding"}}),
        scene({sfx: {src: "ding"}}),
        scene({sfx: {src: "whoosh"}, voice: {src: "boom"}}),
      ),
      {
        preflight: {
          spawn: fakeSpawn((child, call) => {
            const seconds = call.args.join(" ").includes("whoosh") ? 0.6 : call.args.join(" ").includes("boom") ? 0.8 : 1.2;

            decodeSeconds(seconds)(child);
          }, calls),
          ffmpeg: FAKE_FFMPEG,
        },
      },
    );

    assert.equal(calls.length, 3);
    assert.equal(calls.maxActive, 1);
  });

  it("refuses a truncated local MP3 with the file name and its role, from a real ffmpeg decode", async () => {
    const root = await tempDirectory();
    const truncated = fixtureBytes("truncated.mp3");
    const ref = `local:audio:sha256:${sha(truncated)}`;

    await mkdir(join(root, "audio", "takes"), {recursive: true});
    await writeFile(join(root, "audio", "takes", "voice.mp3"), truncated);

    const error = await refuses(
      storyOf(scene(), scene({voice: {src: ref}})),
      {localRoot: root},
      /Audio file takes\/voice\.mp3 cannot be used for rendering\./u,
    );

    assert.match(error.message, /used by: scene 2 voice/u);
    assert.match(error.message, /only 5\.\d\d s of 10\.0\d s could be decoded/u);
  });

  it("renders from the snapshot: editing public/audio after staging cannot change the staged copy", async () => {
    const source = await tempDirectory();

    await cp("public/audio", join(source, "audio"), {recursive: true});

    const original = await readFile(join(source, "audio", "sfx", "ding.wav"));
    const {publicDir, result} = await prepare(storyOf(scene({sfx: {src: "ding"}})), {
      publicSourceDir: source,
    });

    await writeFile(join(source, "audio", "sfx", "ding.wav"), buildWav({seconds: 9}));

    const stagedPath = join(publicDir, ...result.audioSources.ding.path.split("/"));

    assert.equal(sha(await readFile(stagedPath)), sha(original));
    assert.notEqual(
      sha(await readFile(join(source, "audio", "sfx", "ding.wav"))),
      sha(original),
    );
  });

  it("attaches every decode child to the interrupt guard, and an interrupt during a decode is reported as the interrupt", async () => {
    const attached = [];
    const stop = new Error("interrupted");
    let interrupted = false;
    const calls = [];

    await assert.rejects(
      prepare(storyOf(scene({sfx: {src: "ding"}})), {
        control: {attach: (child) => attached.push(child)},
        checkInterrupt: () => {
          if (interrupted) {
            throw stop;
          }
        },
        // The guard killed the decode: ffmpeg exits non-zero while an interrupt is pending.
        preflight: fakePreflight((child) => {
          interrupted = true;
          child.emit("close", null, "SIGINT");
        }, calls),
      }),
      stop,
    );
    assert.equal(attached.length, 1);
    assert.equal(attached[0], calls[0].child);
  });

  it("stops between files when the interrupt check throws", async () => {
    const calls = [];
    const stop = new Error("interrupted");

    await assert.rejects(
      prepare(storyOf(scene({sfx: {src: "ding"}})), {
        checkInterrupt: () => {
          throw stop;
        },
        preflight: fakePreflight(decodeSeconds(1.2), calls),
      }),
      stop,
    );
    assert.equal(calls.length, 0);
  });

  it("stages nothing under bundled/ for a Story whose only audio is a local file", async () => {
    const publicDir = await tempDirectory();
    const root = await tempDirectory();
    const voice = fixtureBytes("tone-440-2.2s.wav");
    const ref = `local:audio:sha256:${sha(voice)}`;

    await mkdir(join(root, "audio"), {recursive: true});
    await writeFile(join(root, "audio", "v.wav"), voice);
    await prepare(storyOf(scene({voice: {src: ref}})), {localRoot: root, publicDir});

    assert.deepEqual(await readdir(join(publicDir, "__local-assets")), ["audio"]);
  });
});
```
